Conditions as of 26 June 2026 as per [ERCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_Response_Coordination_Centre).

Starting in late May 2026, several severe [heatwaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heatwaves) affected [Europe](9239.md). Temperature records were broken in [Austria](26964606.md), [Belarus](3457.md), [Belgium](3343.md), [Czech Republic](5321.md), [Denmark](76972.md), [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Hungary](13275.md), [Ireland](14560.md), [Italy](14532.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md), [Poland](22936.md), [Romania](25445.md), [Slovakia](26830.md), [Slovenia](27338.md), [Spain](26667.md), [Switzerland](26748.md) and the [United Kingdom](31717.md).[^cite_note-21] The first heatwave began on 24 May, with temperatures higher than normal and several [heat-related deaths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat-related_deaths). Temperatures were the highest ever recorded for May,[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-korosec-23] with [climate change](5042951.md) causing the earlier onset of Central Europe's summer.[^cite_note-24] [Western Europe](33800.md) was the most affected area, with reports of record-breaking temperatures in France,[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] Ireland and the United Kingdom. Other countries with unusually high temperatures were Portugal, at 40 °C (104 °F), and Spain, with temperatures in the southwest reaching 39 °C (102 °F).

A second, more severe heatwave began on 17 June, just days before the [summer solstice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_solstice).[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28] In France, Météo-France declared 23 June the country's hottest day since measurements began in 1947, with temperatures reaching 44.3 °C (111.7 °F) in [Pissos](15804244.md), 42.1 °C (107.8 °F) in [Bordeaux](4097.md), and a June record of 40.9 °C (105.6 °F) in Paris.[^cite_note-Kassam-29][^cite_note-30] [In the United Kingdom](83274214.md), temperatures peaked at 38.0 °C (100.4 °F). On 26 June, the [World Meteorological Organization](33584.md) said the heatwave was expected to affect many areas of western, central, and southern Europe over the following two weeks, with the focus of the heat likely to shift towards the [Balkans](4829.md).[^cite_note-wmo-31] By 5 July, over 5,600 [excess deaths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excess_mortality) were reported. Analysis by [World Weather Attribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Weather_Attribution) concluded it was the most severe heatwave ever recorded over the region studied, and that it was "virtually impossible" to explain its greater intensity relative to the [1976 Britain & Ireland heatwave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_British_Isles_heatwave) without accounting for [climate change in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Europe).[^cite_note-32]

A third heatwave in the United Kingdom began on 4 July.[^cite_note-Davanos-33] The [UK Health Security Agency](64959819.md) implemented many heat-health alerts across England.[^cite_note-bbc1-34] The first areas of England officially met heatwave conditions during the morning of 6 July as temperatures in parts of south-eastern England exceeded 28 °C (82 °F) for the third consecutive day; temperatures peaked at 34.0 °C (93.2 °F) in [Teddington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teddington).[^cite_note-35] Temperatures peaked at 35.5 °C (95.9 °F) in [Wisley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisley), Surrey on 9 July, which the Met Office reported was the eighth day above 34 °C (93.2 °F) in 2026, marking a new UK record for the most days exceeding that temperature in a single year.[^cite_note-\:2-36] The length of this heatwave has led to 'extreme' [marine heatwaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_heatwave), affecting the southern [North Sea](21179.md) and parts of the [English Channel](9230.md), which could have potentially negative impacts on marine life.[^cite_note-\:3-37]

The heat also caused many forest fires across Europe.[^cite_note-auto10-38] The heatwaves have also caused [widespread drought across Europe](83908768.md).

The [Elbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbe), [Rhine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhine) and [Danube](21209639.md) experience a severe drought and[^cite_note-39] a record shallowness in the Balkans on 10 August.[^cite_note-40]

Concerns were raised over rivers becoming too hot and shallow for atomic power stations to use in future years.[^cite_note-auto36-41]

Public discussion focused on [climate change adaptation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_adaptation), [net-zero emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net-zero_emissions) goals, and [the role of air conditioning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_air_conditioning) in Europe, where the technology is used much less than in both China and the US.

### Austria

#### May and June

In May, new temperature records were set at 46 [GeoSphere Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GeoSphere_Austria) stations. Of those, [Lienz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lienz) had the highest temperature, recording 33.3 °C (91.9 °F) on 26 May. The city also had a record eight hot days in May, each with a temperature of at least 30 °C (86 °F), beating the previous record of three set in 2001. [Bregenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bregenz) and [Eisenstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eisenstadt) had also set the new record for the highest number of hot days in May. Additionally, May had significantly below-average rainfall, with some regions being between half and two-thirds below the 1991–2020 average. May was also the fifth sunniest on record.[^cite_note-48]

On 18 June, GeoSphere Austria announced that a heatwave was imminent, with highs between 30 and 36 °C (86 and 97 °F) expected to persist through the end of the month.[^cite_note-49] On 20 June, temperatures reached 35 °C (95 °F) at five GeoSphere Austria stations and a temperature of 36.0 °C (96.8 °F) was recorded in [Bad Deutsch-Altenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Deutsch-Altenburg), marking the first time the value had been reached in Austria in 2026.[^cite_note-50] On 21 June, GeoSphere Austria recorded a temperature of at least 35.0 °C (95.0 °F) at 18 stations in its network, which included a temperature of 36.6 °C (97.9 °F) in Bad Deutsch-Altenburg and a temperature of 36.1 °C (97.0 °F) at a further two stations.[^cite_note-51]

On 24 June, GeoSphere Austria announced that temperatures were expected to exceed 35 °C (95 °F) in all state capitals and reach 39 °C (102 °F) in [Vienna](55866.md) on both 27 and 28 June. As a result, the highest heat warning would be in effect for much of northeastern Austria, including Vienna, as well as for the southern cities of [Graz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graz), [Klagenfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klagenfurt) and [Villach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villach), whilst the second-highest heat warning would be in effect for other parts of the country.[^cite_note-52] The [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Austrian_Grand_Prix), a [Formula One](10854.md) race taking place at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring) on 28 June, was declared a heat hazard by the [FIA](248019.md) as temperatures at the circuit were expected to reach 36 °C (97 °F) during the race.[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-54]

On 27 June, a temperature of 39.3 °C (102.7 °F) was measured in Bad Deutsch-Altenburg, breaking a previous national June temperature record that was set in 2013, when a temperature of 38.6 °C (101.5 °F) was measured in [Waidhofen an der Ybbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waidhofen_an_der_Ybbs).[^cite_note-orf-27jun-55] The same day, new June temperature records were set in six out of nine [states of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_of_Austria), with the previous national June temperature record also surpassed in Vienna and [Innsbruck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innsbruck), where temperatures reached 38.8 °C (101.8 °F) and 38.7 °C (101.7 °F), respectively. A temperature of 38.2 °C (100.8 °F) was measured in [Bad Goisern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Goisern_am_Hallst%C3%A4ttersee), whilst Eisenstadt reached 37.9 °C (100.2 °F).[^cite_note-56]

On 28 June, temperatures exceeded 39 °C (102 °F) at several stations in [Lower Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Austria) and Vienna, as June temperature records were broken nationwide at 102 of 251 stations that have been active for more than 15 years. That day, a temperature of 40.0 °C (104.0 °F) was measured in the [Innere Stadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innere_Stadt) district of Vienna, which set a new June temperature record for Austria and marked the first time the value had been reached in the capital since records began. Outside Vienna, a temperature of 39.9 °C (103.8 °F) was measured in the Langenlebarn area of [Tulln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulln_an_der_Donau), Innsbruck reached 38.7 °C (101.7 °F) for the second consecutive day, [Linz](55856.md) was one of three places in [Upper Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Austria) where a temperature of 38.2 °C (100.8 °F) was measured, alongside [Enns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enns_\(town\)) and [Wels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wels), and the same temperature was recorded in Eisenstadt, the state capital of [Burgenland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgenland).[^cite_note-uwz-28jun-57][^cite_note-orf-28jun-58]

On 29 June, it was reported that nighttime temperatures did not drop below 20 °C (68 °F) at 90 of 280 stations nationwide and a station in the [Ottakring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottakring) district of Vienna did not register temperatures below 27.3 °C (81.1 °F), which set a new June record for the highest nighttime temperature since the beginning of record-keeping in Austria.[^cite_note-59] The same day, highs in excess of 38 °C (100 °F) were recorded at several stations, with a temperature of 40.1 °C (104.2 °F) measured in Bad Deutsch-Altenburg, which surpassed the national June temperature record set in Vienna the day before and marked the first time since the beginning of record-keeping that a temperature in excess of 40 °C (104 °F) had been measured in Austria on two days.[^cite_note-uwz-29jun-60][^cite_note-orf-29jun-61]

The heatwave lasted 14 days and ended on 1 July, with severe thunderstorms affecting parts of northeastern Austria that day.[^cite_note-62] During the heatwave, which was the longest to affect Austria in June, new local temperature records were set at 60 of 251 stations that have been active for more than 15 years, whilst an additional 88 of the 251 stations set new June temperature records for their respective locations. At the state level, June temperature records were broken in all Austrian states except [Carinthia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carinthia). Historically, this June was more than 2.5 °C (4.5 °F) warmer than the 1991–2020 average for Austria and the fifth-warmest on record.[^cite_note-uwz-30jun-63]

#### July and August

On 29 July, a new heatwave began in Austria, the second of the year nationwide. In western and southern Austria, the heatwave was the third of the year, after an early heatwave that began on 24 May and lasted between five and nine days. It was initially expected to reach its peak on 31 July and, similarly to the June heatwave, be most intense in the lowlands of eastern Austria, where temperatures were predicted to reach between 38 and 40 °C (100 and 104 °F).[^cite_note-standard-29jul-64] Drought was felt in various regions of the country and there was a heightened risk of wildfires associated with it, with such conditions mainly resulting from the fact that, as of 27 July, the amount of precipitation since December 2025 was approximately 30 percent lower than the average needed to prevent drought.[^cite_note-standard-29jul-64] As of 29 July, there were just three of 2,114 [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_\(Austria\)) in Austria that did not experience at least one day with a temperature of 30 °C (86 °F) or higher, with several municipalities experiencing more than 30 such "heat days" in 2026.[^cite_note-65]

On 31 July, GeoSphere Austria measured a temperature of 40.3 °C (104.5 °F) in [Wieselburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wieselburg) in Lower Austria, which set a new national record for the highest July temperature, surpassing a 1983 record of 39.7 °C (103.5 °F) in [Dellach im Drautal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dellach_im_Drautal) in Carinthia. It also set a new July temperature record for the state of Lower Austria for the first time since 1957, when a temperature of 39.4 °C (102.9 °F) was measured in [Horn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horn,_Austria). At the state level, July temperature records were also broken in Vienna and Upper Austria, as temperatures exceeded 39 °C (102 °F) at several GeoSphere Austria stations. Preliminary data suggested that at least 30 of these stations set a new yearly record.[^cite_note-orf-31jul-66]

On 3 August, GeoSphere Austria measured a temperature of 40.1 °C (104.2 °F) in the [Stammersdorf](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stammersdorf) area of Vienna, marking the second time the value had been reached in the capital in 2026 and setting a new temperature record for the location.[^cite_note-uwz-3aug-67][^cite_note-68] The service measured temperatures in excess of 38 °C (100 °F) at several of its stations in northeastern Austria, including a temperature of 40.0 °C (104.0 °F) in the Langenlebarn area of Tulln, with Eisenstadt and [St. Pölten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._P%C3%B6lten) both reaching 38.8 °C (101.8 °F) as the highest temperature for a state capital outside Vienna.[^cite_note-uwz-3aug-67]

On 4 August, a temperature of 41.0 °C (105.8 °F) was measured at the aforementioned station in the Stammersdorf area of Vienna, which broke a 2013 record of 40.5 °C (104.9 °F) in Bad Deutsch-Altenburg for the highest temperature measured in Austria since records began. It was one of nine GeoSphere Austria stations to measure a temperature in excess of 40 °C (104 °F) that day.[^cite_note-uwz-4aug-69][^cite_note-70] The record lasted just one day, as the service measured a temperature of 41.2 °C (106.2 °F) at its station in Bad Deutsch-Altenburg on 5 August. It was among five stations in its network to register a temperature in excess of 40 °C (104 °F) that day. Also among the five stations was the one in the Stammersdorf area of Vienna, which measured a temperature of 41.0 °C (105.8 °F) for the second consecutive day.[^cite_note-uwz-5aug-71][^cite_note-orf-5aug-72]

On 10 August, water levels along the Danube in Austria were reported to be significantly lower than typical, leading to the cancellation of around 30 percent of all Austrian [cruises](314855.md) along the river.[^cite_note-73] Water levels in the Danube measured at a station in [Hainburg an der Donau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hainburg_an_der_Donau) were the lowest since the station began operations in 1976, with the lowest values for both July and August being significantly lower than those recorded both during the [2003 European heatwave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_European_heatwave) and on multiple occasions during autumn and winter, when water levels in the Danube in Austria typically reach their yearly lows.[^cite_note-74] As a result of the drought that had intensified after the June heatwave and persisted into August, farmers warned of a significantly reduced harvest. In late July, the [Austrian Chamber of Agriculture](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landwirtschaftskammer%20%C3%96sterreich) expected a decrease of 15 to 20 percent in the grain harvest compared to the previous year and one of the worst harvests in the last 10 years.[^cite_note-75] In mid-August, estimates by [AgrarMarkt Austria](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agrarmarkt%20Austria) suggested a decrease of 19 percent in the grain harvest for 2026 compared to 2025.[^cite_note-76] On 11 August, it was reported that the climate data since 1961, available to GeoSphere Austria, indicated the longest and most consistent yearly deficits in precipitation for eastern Austria in the last 65 years.[^cite_note-77]

On 13 August, it was reported that there were approximately 270 forest fires in Austria in 2026, representing an increase of almost 50 percent compared to the previous year and burning approximately five times more area than during an average year, as researchers at the [International Institute for Applied Systems Analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Applied_Systems_Analysis) (IIASA) warned of forest fires becoming more common in Austria in the coming years due to climate change. At the time, a forest fire near [Wiener Neustadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiener_Neustadt) required several days of firefighting efforts to be extinguished.[^cite_note-78]

On 16 August, temperatures in excess of 36 °C (97 °F) were still being recorded at several stations in northeastern Austria, including Vienna, as the heatwave that had begun on 29 July was expected to end with the arrival of a cold front the following day.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80]

On 30 August, it was reported that several stations in the GeoSphere Austria network had recorded temperatures in excess of 30 °C (86 °F) on more than 50 days in 2026. At the time, the service's station in [Ferlach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferlach) had recorded such temperatures on 59 days in 2026, followed by Bad Deutsch-Altenburg with 58 "heat days" during the year. Both stations surpassed a 2024 record of 57 such days in Bad Deutsch-Altenburg. The summer of 2026 marked the first time since the beginning of record-keeping that a temperature of 40 °C (104 °F) had been measured in Austria during each of the three summer months (June, July and August).[^cite_note-81]

### Belarus

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

The air temperature in the south of Belarus was predicted to reach +30-31 °C on 14 July.[^cite_note-82] Belhydromet predicted heavy thunderstorms and issued a yellow alert in response.[^cite_note-83]

For the first time in history, the temperature in Belarus rose above 40 °C (104 °F). This historical maximum was reported in the town of [Pinsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinsk), with a recorded temperature of 40.4 °C (104.7 °F) between 2\:00 pm to 2\:20 pm on 29 June, as reported by the Republican Centre for Hydrometeorology, Control of Radioactive Contamination and Environmental Monitoring.[^cite_note-84]

On 6 August, the air temperature rose above 40 °C for the second time in the country's history in the town of [Mazyr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazyr), where the temperature reached 40.3 °C (104.5 °F).[^cite_note-85]

### Belgium

A heatwave is reached when temperatures reach at least 25 degrees for 5 days in a row, with at least 3 of those days reaching 30 degrees or more in [Uccle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uccle).[^cite_note-86]

Between 23 and 25 May, [Uccle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uccle) recorded temperatures of over 28 °C (82 °F). Locally in [Brussels](3708.md), temperatures peaked 31 °C (88 °F) on 25 May.[^cite_note-87] On 26 May, Uccle broke the daily temperature record set in 1985 and peaked at 30.3 °C (86.5 °F).[^cite_note-88]

The heatwave in the second half of June brought higher temperatures. Between 20 and 25 June, temperatures in Uccle reached 35.3 °C (95.5 °F). Forecasts predicted even higher temperatures, but it never exceeded that peak.[^cite_note-89] On 24 June, Belgium set a price record of over €1 per [kWh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KWh) electricity at [sunset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset), as traditional power stations were maxed out to cope with increased [air conditioning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_conditioning).[^cite_note-90]

On 26 June, the annual [Battle of Waterloo reenactment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Waterloo_reenactment) was cancelled.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-Ahmed-92] On 27 June, [Katy Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katy_Perry)'s performance at the [Werchter Boutique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Werchter_Boutique) festival was cancelled due to severe thunderstorms in the Benelux area.[^cite_note-93]

Between 18 and 29 June, the excess mortality rate in the country was 39 percent, with 1,222 excess deaths reported during the heatwave.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] A report by [Sciensano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sciensano) published on 8 July reported 1.747 excess deaths between 18 June and 1 July, with the excess mortality rate climbing to 48 percent. Making it the deadliest Belgian heatwave in the last 30 years.[^cite_note-96]

While temperatures didn't go below 25 degrees between 6 and 17 July, a heatwave was not reached in Belgium as only 2 days above 30 degrees were recorded with the [RMI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Meteorological_Institute) recording 29,9 degrees on Saturday 11 July.[^cite_note-97]

### Bosnia and Herzegovina

#### June heatwave

FHMZ forecasted very high temperatures in Bosnia by the end of June 2026 projecting it as high as 41 °C (106 °F).[^cite_note-98] On 28 June, [Mostar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostar) recorded a temperature of 41 °C (106 °F), making it the highest national temperature of the month in 2026.[^cite_note-Klix-99] However, an unofficial reading recorded the temperature as high as 50 °C (122 °F), mainly on asphalt surface.[^cite_note-100] The unofficial temperature was reached in 2012,[^cite_note-101] 2017,[^cite_note-102] 2022,[^cite_note-103] and 2024.[^cite_note-104] The temperature in Mostar the day prior was 39 °C (102 °F).[^cite_note-105]

#### July–August heatwave

After a cooling period of rain and thunderstorms, another heatwave struck Bosnia on 30 July in which [Mostar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostar) and [Široki Brijeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iroki_Brijeg) recorded a temperature of 42 °C (108 °F).[^cite_note-\:1-106] On the same day, [Trebinje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trebinje) recorded a temperature of 39.6 °C (103.3 °F) breaking the previous record set in 2000.[^cite_note-107] Projections estimated that in the week from 30 July to 5 August, temperatures would go as high as 43 °C (109 °F).[^cite_note-108] On 31 July, six cities recorded temperatures above 35 °C (95 °F) with Mostar recording a temperature above 40 °C (104 °F) for the second time consecutively.[^cite_note-109] New projections estimated temperatures as high as 44 °C (111 °F).[^cite_note-110] On 1 August, Mostar recorded a 41 °C (106 °F) temperature for the second time consecutively and was for the third day consecutively above 40 °C (104 °F).[^cite_note-111] On 4 August, Mostar recorded 40 °C (104 °F) as it was on its sixth day consecutively above the temperature.[^cite_note-112] On the same day, five cities were above 35 °C (95 °F).[^cite_note-113] On 5 August, Mostar was above 40 °C (104 °F) for seven days consecutively.[^cite_note-114] The day after, 11 cities recorded temperatures above 35 °C (95 °F) with Mostar recording a temperature of 43 °C (109 °F) as the city was for nine days above 40 °C (104 °F).[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-\:5-116] Mostar also had the highest morning temperature recorded at 30 °C (86 °F) at 8\:00 AM.[^cite_note-117]

#### August heatwave

In late August, another heatwave struck Bosnia with the highest morning temperature recorded in [Gradačac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grada%C4%8Dac) at 31 °C (88 °F), followed by a subsequent new all-time high record of 41 °C (106 °F), breaking the previous one set in 24 July, 2007.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119] Projections estimated local temperature as high as 42 °C (108 °F) by the end of the month.[^cite_note-120] During the heatwave, [dust from Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saharan_dust) engulfed Bosnia, including [Sarajevo](26786.md). Mostar recorded a temperature of 39 °C (102 °F) on 27 August.[^cite_note-121]

#### Subsequent fires

Multiple regions in Bosnia were hit by fires as a result of the heat. Between 30 and 31 July, fires broke out in [Široki Brijeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iroki_Brijeg), [Teslić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesli%C4%87), and near [Banja Luka](385109.md). Local residents were instructed to remain on high alert to the increased risk of fires.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124] On 23 August, a fire broke out on [Trebević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trebevi%C4%87) near Sarajevo.[^cite_note-125] After it was put under control, it broke out again on 26 August.[^cite_note-126] One person was evacuated during the fire.[^cite_note-127] The fire was finally put out on 12 September but local news said that the damage would be visible for years.[^cite_note-128]

#### Water supply crisis in Sarajevo

Following persistent irrational consumption of water in the midst of the heatwave, KJKP ViK Sarajevo, on 20 August, formed special teams to patrol households in order to stabilize the water supply.[^cite_note-129] As a result of fuzzy waters and low yield in Mošćanica and Crnil springs, on 30 August, ViK Sarajevo imposed regulated daily water supply cuts from 1\:00 AM to 5\:00 AM and from 11\:00 AM to 4\:00 PM.[^cite_note-130] Locals complained about the lack of information sent in advance by ViK Sarajevo about the sudden water supply cuts.[^cite_note-131] Despite the implemented measures, water level remained critically low throughout September with alarming reports stating that the Bačevo spring, which supplies over 400,000 citizens in Sarajevo, had both wells recorded low water levels, the first one recording 3.12 meters while the second one recorded 1.17 meters.[^cite_note-132] ViK Sarajevo estimated that it would take weeks of raining for the water supply to be restored at a normal level.[^cite_note-133] Reports indicated on continued irrational consumption despite the regulatory measures.[^cite_note-134] On 21 September, one of the wells in Bačevo was recorded only two centimeters above the critical level despite the cooler weather.[^cite_note-135] Locals from Jarčedoli complained that they were out of water for five days and that the water truck had only come once, forcing them to use their own canisters of water as a result.[^cite_note-136]

Water supply cuts have varied in their time periods, with some night time cuts lasting until 6\:00 AM.[^cite_note-137] Reports also indicated a huge portion of an aging water pipeline with up to 80% of the water pipes as old as 60 years, exposing a lack of maintenance of the water supply pipeline which poses a risk to Sarajevo.[^cite_note-138] Water supply cuts have resulted in yellowish or brown water appearing in households. ViK instructed to have all tap water running for a few minutes until the color was to change back to normal.[^cite_note-139]

### Bulgaria

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

Wfy24 meteorologists reported that the [Danubian Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danubian_Plain_\(Bulgaria\)) in Bulgaria was approaching 38 °C (100 °F) during late June 2026.[^cite_note-140]

Bulgaria was predicted on 26 June 2026, to hit by temperatures of up to 38 °C over the next week and a Code Red heat alert was issued that day.[^cite_note-141]

Temperatures were between 32C and 37C on 30 July 2026 and were expected to reach 43C that weekend.[^cite_note-auto28-142] The National Institute of Meteorology and Hydrology (NIMH) recorded temperatures of 40C on 3 August and predicted 42-43C for the next few days.[^cite_note-auto28-142]

#### Subsequent fires

Many of them were accidents or neglectfully used portable barbeques, but many were considered to be arson.

Devastating forest fires, mostly caused by arsonists, swept the country on 23 July 2026.[^cite_note-143]

Fires still raged around Sliven, Burgas, Plovdiv, Stara Zagora, Yambol, Haskovo, Blagoevgrad and Kyustendil Bulgaria on 27 July 2025.[^cite_note-144]

Several social media posts, epically on X condemned the fire raising and arson attacks on 1 August.[^cite_note-auto24-145]

### Croatia

Croatia experienced a heatwave in the second half of June. In its weather forecast for 19 June, the [Croatian Meteorological and Hydrological Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Meteorological_and_Hydrological_Service) (DHMZ) reported that temperatures were expected to reach 32 °C (90 °F) in central Croatia and up to 34 °C (93 °F) in some areas along the coast, with sunny and hot weather likely to continue for several days. In coastal areas, temperatures were expected to remain between 21 and 24 °C (70 and 75 °F) during the night as well.[^cite_note-146]

On 26 June, DHMZ issued a red alert for much of Croatia's coast, reporting that temperatures were likely to reach 34 °C (93 °F) in most of the country and up to 36 °C (97 °F) in its southern parts, and expecting the heatwave to reach its peak by 28 June, when highs of 39 °C (102 °F) were predicted to occur in the country's interior. In coastal areas, temperatures were expected to remain above 25 °C (77 °F) during the night as well.[^cite_note-147]

On 28 June, DHMZ recorded highs in excess of 36 °C (97 °F) in many parts of the country, both along the coast and in the interior, with a temperature of 37.5 °C (99.5 °F) measured in [Imotski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imotski), followed by 37.0 °C (98.6 °F) in [Senj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senj).[^cite_note-vl-28jun-148] On 29 June, temperatures rose to between 36 and 38 °C (97 and 100 °F) in several cities around the country, [Osijek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osijek) reached 38 °C (100 °F), following multiple days on which highs in excess of 35 °C (95 °F) had been recorded in eastern Croatia, whilst a temperature of 39 °C (102 °F) was measured in both Senj and [Cres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cres_\(town\)).[^cite_note-hrt-29jun-149]

On 30 June, temperatures reached 39.5 °C (103.1 °F) in [Split](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Split,_Croatia), which broke a July 1950 record of 38.6 °C (101.5 °F) for the highest temperature measured in the city, as local June temperature records were also broken in [Šibenik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0ibenik) and [Zadar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zadar). During the same day, a temperature of 41.0 °C (105.8 °F) was measured in [Knin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knin), surpassing a 2022 record of 40.4 °C (104.7 °F) for the highest June temperature measured in the city.[^cite_note-jl-30jun-150][^cite_note-vl-30jun-151] Temperatures in excess of 38 °C (100 °F) were recorded in many places along the coast, as well as in eastern Croatia, with highs of 39.8 °C (103.6 °F) at [Osijek Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osijek_Airport) and 38.8 °C (101.8 °F) in the city of Osijek, respectively.[^cite_note-vl-30jun-151][^cite_note-hrt-30jun-152] Late in the afternoon, a storm caused by the heat affected parts of [Dalmatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalmatia).[^cite_note-hrt-30jun-152]

On 1 July, temperatures were expected to reach between 32 and 37 °C (90 and 99 °F), as thunderstorms began to occur in many parts of Croatia in the afternoon, eventually ending the heatwave that, according to DHMZ, had begun on 20 June in northern parts of the Croatian coast.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154]

Another heatwave in Croatia began on 30 July, when the highest heat warning was in effect for [Rijeka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rijeka) and the surrounding region. Hot and sunny weather was expected to affect most of the country in the coming days and persist into the first week of August, with temperatures predicted to reach between 35 and 40 °C (95 and 104 °F) on 1 August, and stay around 25 °C (77 °F) or higher overnight in some areas, especially along the coast.[^cite_note-155] According to a weather forecast published on 30 July, the heatwave was likely to last at least one week. Temperatures were expected to reach 37 °C (99 °F) in eastern Croatia on 31 July, with highs between 33 and 38 °C (91 and 100 °F) predicted to occur along and near the coast the same day. Highs of 40 °C (104 °F) were likely to occur in the [Dalmatian Hinterland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalmatian_Hinterland) on both 1 and 2 August.[^cite_note-156]

On 6 August, the heatwave reached its peak in eastern Croatia, with a temperature of 40.0 °C (104.0 °F) measured at Osijek Airport.[^cite_note-157] At the time, it was also reported that the waters in the [Drava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drava) and the Danube in the region had fallen to the lowest levels for years, mainly due to a combination of long-standing heat and a lack of precipitation.[^cite_note-158]

On 11 and 12 August, intense heat spread along the Croatian coast, with several stations setting new local temperature records, including a temperature of 39.8 °C (103.6 °F) in Senj and temperatures of 40.1 °C (104.2 °F) and 40.2 °C (104.4 °F) in [Dubrovnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubrovnik) and Split, respectively.[^cite_note-159]

#### Subsequent fires

Late on 28 June, a forest fire broke out on the island of [Vis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vis_\(island\)), requiring an all-night firefighting operation with 15 vehicles and 70 firefighters. On the morning of 29 June, four aircraft arrived to assist in the firefighting operation and the fire was localised during the same day. Approximately 300 ha (740 acres) of pine forest and [macchia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maquis_shrubland) were affected, although no inhabited buildings were damaged.[^cite_note-160]

On 30 June, fires broke out on the island of [Čiovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%8Ciovo) and near [Orebić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orebi%C4%87) on the [Pelješac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelje%C5%A1ac) peninsula as a result of lightning strikes during thunderstorms in the region.[^cite_note-161] The following day, it was reported that the two fires were under control. However, there were several other fires in southern Croatia and firefighters warned of an increased number of wildfires compared to the previous year.[^cite_note-162] The fire on the island of Čiovo reignited on 2 July and was not declared extinguished until the morning of 11 July. By then, it had burnt approximately 120 ha (300 acres) of landscape covered with grass, macchia and pine forests.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164]

On 4 July, a fire broke out on the island of [Hvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hvar), affecting approximately 200 ha (490 acres) of landscape covered with grass, macchia and pine forests, and requiring an extensive firefighting operation involving dozens of vehicles and four aircraft.[^cite_note-165] The following day, it was reported that the fire was under control. It was the second fire to affect the island in two weeks and one of its most devastating fires in approximately a decade.[^cite_note-166]

On 11 July, two fires affected a pine forest on the island of [Korčula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C4%8Dula). They merged into a single fire later that day, prompting an extensive firefighting operation overnight. Approximately 170 firefighters were initially involved. They received assistance from four firefighting aircraft on the morning of 12 July.[^cite_note-167] On 13 July, it was reported that the fire was under control after it had burnt almost 900 ha (2,200 acres) of mostly forested landscape and spread over an area approximately 4 km (2.5 miles) wide.[^cite_note-168]

On the evening of 13 August, strong winds caused a fire to affect approximately 1,000 ha (2,500 acres) of landscape in just four hours and quickly spread towards the town of [Omiš](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omi%C5%A1), leading to extensive damage to buildings, destroying numerous vehicles and prompting the evacuation of approximately 1,200 residents and tourists in the area. On 15 August, the fire was reported to be under control, leaving at least one person dead and dozens injured, of whom eight people were hospitalised with life-threatening burns.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170]

On 14 August, a fire broke out on the island of [Dugi Otok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dugi_Otok), burning approximately 300 ha (740 acres) of forest and macchia before it was localised during the afternoon of 15 August.[^cite_note-171]

Record-keeping had begun in 1874.[^cite_note-Ali-175] The temperature reached 37.0 °C (98.6 °F) in [Ødum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%98dum?action=edit\&redlink=1), north of [Aarhus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aarhus) and in Beldringe, north of Odense.[^cite_note-DMI2026-176]

### France



Paris, 26 June at 17\:28. A pharmacy's thermometer sign shows 44 °C (111 °F).

In May, there were reports of record-breaking temperatures.[^cite_note-182] The national average was 24.9 °C (76.8 °F) on 26 May, the hottest May on record. The [heat dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_dome) continued with [Angoulême](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angoul%C3%AAme)-[La Couronne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Couronne,_Charente) recording a temperature of 37.8 °C (100.0 °F) on 28 May.[^cite_note-183] Seven heat-related deaths occurred in May.[^cite_note-184]

Beginning on 22 June, overnight temperatures were the hottest since records began in 1947, and [Météo-France](4732627.md) issued a heatwave red alert for [54 departments](38523.md). The nationwide heat index, an average of the measurements from 30 weather stations of the day and nighttime highs, provisionally reached 29.8 °C (85.6 °F). The single highest temperature was 44.3 °C (111.7 °F) in [Pissos](15804244.md), with parts of Western France above 40 °C (104 °F). [Bordeaux](4097.md) reached 41.1 °C (106.0 °F). Nearly half of [metropolitan France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_France) was affected.[^cite_note-Kassam-29]



Average maximum temperatures for June 2026 in metropolitan France, and difference with the average maximum temperatures for June between 1991 and 2020.

Drowning deaths in France markedly increased.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-Moses-186] On 26 June, Sports Minister [Marina Ferrari](73129422.md) stated in an interview with *[Le Figaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Figaro)* that two-thirds of the 55 people that had drowned had been swimming in unsupervised or unauthorized areas,[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-Ahmed-92][^cite_note-Moses-186] and emergency services reported 61% more calls than the previous week, and 75% more than the same time last year.[^cite_note-Le_Monde-188] The historic [Canal Saint-Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canal_Saint-Martin) in the centre of Paris was opened to public swimming; one man in an unsupervised area was found unresponsive.[^cite_note-189] Deaths include [En Avant Guingamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/En_Avant_Guingamp) footballer Kenzo Kies.[^cite_note-190] At least 18 died from related causes, including two children left in a hot car.[^cite_note-191] The heat forced 845 junior and middle schools to close, and a further 1,800 to adjust their teaching schedules.[^cite_note-192] Around 68,000 households in France experienced power outages.[^cite_note-193]

By 24 June, two-thirds of France was under a red alert.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-Krupa-1-195] On 25 June, 72 of the 96 Metropolitan France departments—75%—were under red alert. Paris mayor [Emmanuel Grégoire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Gr%C3%A9goire) announced (without giving specific figures) that nearly all of the "critical indicators" were up: calls to [emergency medical services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_services) and [fire brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_brigade), [ER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_room) admissions, and deaths.[^cite_note-Krupa-2-196] Two nuclear power plants were shut down,[^cite_note-Krupa-2-196] and later a third. On 25 June, the [French Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Ministry_of_Health) announced that 44 [cardiac arrests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_arrest) had occurred in Paris on 24 June, and that there had been a fourfold increase in ER visits for heat-related reasons nationwide.[^cite_note-Carrington-6][^cite_note-197]



Number of days with temperatures of 40°C or higher in 2026 (up to August 27). Many weather stations, particularly in western France, recorded more days at 40°C or higher in 2026 than during the entire 1975–2025 period.

In the morning of 25 June, a rare [heat burst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_burst) event was observed at [Belle-Île](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belle_%C3%8Ele). Temperature rapidly increased from 26 °C (79 °F) to 34 °C (93 °F) when the high-altitude air of the dissipating thunderstorm crashed down at over 100 km/h (62 mph). The whole fluctuation took place in the span of less than an hour.[^cite_note-198]

On 26 June, [Enedis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enedis) stated that 50,000 of its clients were without power.[^cite_note-Le_Monde-188] In Paris, police chief [Patrice Faure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrice_Faure) stated that emergency services were overwhelmed by heat-related illnesses, with the head of the Association of French Emergency Doctors specifying that 55 people had died under emergency care in 24 hours, the normal number being three or five.[^cite_note-199] [SAMU](9581361.md) announced that 109 people have died in Paris in 24 hours, against seven normally at this time of the year.[^cite_note-200]

The city announced restrictions on alcohol from 26 to 28 June, with public consumption of alcohol (excluding outdoor seating areas at licensed establishments) prohibited from 12\:00 to 07\:00, and sales of takeaway alcohol prohibited from 18\:00 through the following morning from Friday through Sunday.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] [Paris Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Pride) (which was postponed to September) and the [Solidays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidays) music festival were cancelled following requests by [Paris Police Prefecture](1386763.md),[^cite_note-203] along with [Ironman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ironman_Triathlon) Nice.[^cite_note-204] [World Athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Athletics) stated that the [Diamond League](21778161.md) [Meeting de Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meeting_de_Paris) would still go on, but with non-professional events cancelled, and adjusted start times among other measures agreed upon by the prefecture. The [Top 14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Top_14_season) rugby union final was also expected to go on, but with alcohol sales prohibited at [Stade de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_de_France).[^cite_note-205]

On 28 June, the French national health authority ([Haute Autorité de santé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haute_Autorit%C3%A9_de_sant%C3%A9?action=edit\&redlink=1)) announced that 1,000 additional deaths were recorded compared to the totals for previous months, a figure expected to rise,[^cite_note-lemonde-1-206] and the interior minister, [Laurent Nuñez](60527935.md), noted that the ambulance services had responded to more than 122,000 calls during the height of the heatwave. Those 65 and older and/or [shut-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recluse) had the highest rise in death rates. Finally, severe lightning storms hit much of northern France in the immediate aftermath of the heatwave;[^cite_note-Krupa-4-207][^cite_note-208] wildlife also suffered.[^cite_note-209] An estimate by Dr Christopher Callahan, a climate scientist at [Indiana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University), found that the heatwave had killed more than 5,000 people in France between 22 and 28 June.[^cite_note-guardiantoll-9] 10,000 excess mortality deaths have been recorded between June 17 and July 2.[^cite_note-210]



Average maximum temperatures for July 2026 in metropolitan France, and difference with the average maximum temperatures for July between 1991 and 2020.

Between 24 and 26 June the temperature hit 40 °C (104 °F). The previous temperature records, with measurements starting from the 19th century, were in 1947, 2019, and 2022. One [deputy mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_mayor) of Paris, [Audrey Pulvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audrey_Pulvar), noted that the assumption had been that such heat could be possible in 2030, following two abnormally warm decades.[^cite_note-211]

In mid-July during a third heatwave the Louvre, the Eiffel tower, and other tourist destinations again curtailed their hours, and the [Tour de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_de_France) shorted a portion of its racing route through hilly terrain, the first time in its history it had taken such a measure. Of the nation's departments, 24 were under the maximum heat alert.[^cite_note-auto2-212]

#### Subsequent fires

The heatwaves have heightened the risks of wildfires, with some starting in the north of France where wildfires are rarer, such as [Fontainebleau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fontainebleau).[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214]

Forest fires hit the [departement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Departements_of_France) of [Gironde](64654.md) on 5 August detonating some unexploded [World War II](32927.md) ordnance around the village of [Le Porge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Porge) due to radial heat in the soil (the [Goff Effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goff_Effect_\(thermodynamics\)?action=edit\&redlink=1)), while others were safely disposed of by the [Bordeaux Explosive Ordnance Disposal Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bordeaux_Explosive_Ordnance_Disposal_Service?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-215]

Outside [Montpellier](73369.md) and in the municipality of [La Tieule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Tieule), 200 firefighters fought forest fires on 9 August 2026.[^cite_note-216]

### Germany

#### May

Temperatures rose above 30 °C (86 °F) for the first time in 2026 on 23 May.[^cite_note-217] Increased cooling demands from the heatwave combined with the usage of more expensive power sources due to lower wind power generation caused day-ahead power prices to jump by 29% on 27 May.[^cite_note-218]

Many weather stations recorded new records for the number of days ≥30 °C (86 °F), especially in the southern half of the country. In the [Upper Rhine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Rhine) region, six to seven hot days were recorded locally. [Wutöschingen-Ofteringen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wut%C3%B6schingen), [Baden-Württemberg](66401.md), recorded nine hot days in May, more than double the previous record of four set in May 2005.[^cite_note-219] The highest temperature recorded in Germany in May 2026 was 34 °C (93 °F) in [Regensburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regensburg), [Bavaria](3764.md), on 26 May.[^cite_note-220]

#### June



A woman in [high-visibility clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-visibility_clothing) using a [blowtorch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blowtorch) to clean and repair [bitumen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitumen), a component of the tram track, that was damaged by the heatwave in [Leipzig](17955.md), Germany, in June 2026

Germany experienced another heatwave in the second half of June. Starting in the southwest, on 17 June temperatures already exceeded 30 °C (86 °F) at 25 weather stations, with a maximum of 32.7 °C (90.9 °F) at [Müllheim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BCllheim), while two days later the heat was spreading over 441 stations (out of 505) with a maximum of 38.5 °C (101.3 °F) in [Kitzingen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitzingen).[^cite_note-221] On 20 and 21 June, it was reported that temperatures were expected to reach between 32 and 39 °C (90 and 102 °F) in central and southern Germany.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] According to a weather forecast published on 23 June, the ongoing heatwave was likely to continue for at least one week as temperatures were expected to reach above 35 °C (95 °F) in most of the country by 26 June, with highs around 40 °C (104 °F) expected in the southwest.[^cite_note-224] On 25 June, the national railway network [Deutsche Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bahn) offered its clientele free cancellation of reservations made before 23 June,[^cite_note-Krupa-2-196][^cite_note-225] and later advised against travel in general due to the high risk, specifically from [wildfires](56106.md), heavy summer rain, and thunderstorms.[^cite_note-Carrington-6]

One day later, 26 June, a new all-time national record high of 41.3 °C (106.3 °F) was measured in [Saarbrücken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saarbr%C3%BCcken)[^cite_note-226] and already 23 weather stations were hitting the old record for June, which was measured on 30 June 2019 in [Bernburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernburg) with 39.6 °C (103.3 °F).[^cite_note-227] This all-time record was broken again on 27 June in [Drewitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drewitz), part of [Möckern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%B6ckern), in the northern part of [Saxony-Anhalt](38138.md) reaching 41.8 °C (107.2 °F).[^cite_note-228]

By 27 June, 13 of the 16 German states had broken their all-time temperature records.[^cite_note-229] National Express suspended its regional railway service in North Rhine-Westphalia state for six hours, and more generally only essential travel was advised. Furthermore, the concrete in several areas of Germany's [autobahn](1764276.md) system expanded and burst open, leading to road closures for repair[^cite_note-Ali-175] and also the tram network in [Leipzig](17955.md) had to suspend their services because of melting [asphalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asphalt_concrete).[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] Near [Hamburg](13467.md) the main traffic lane of the [Bundesautobahn 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bundesautobahn_7) (A7) was closed because the asphalt had split.[^cite_note-232] A motorway in [Brandenburg](3765.md) and Saxony-Anhalt was closed on 30 June due to [heat damage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_expansion).[^cite_note-233] About 160 people had to be evacuated from a nursing home in Dormagen the night to Saturday 26 June, after temperatures reached 35 °C (95 °F) in the building. Two people died, and the incident is under investigation.[^cite_note-234]

On 28 June, temperatures reached 41.7 °C (107.1 °F) in Coschen, close to the Polish border.[^cite_note-Krupa-4-207] In Gohrischheide, [Saxony](28395.md),[^cite_note-235] a [wildfire](56106.md) broke out in a forested [nature reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_reserve) contaminated with munitions from [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-Krupa-4-207][^cite_note-236] Police in Berlin fired [water cannons](431462.md) in the air to cool people off.[^cite_note-Krupa-4-207] According to a final report by the [German Weather Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Weather_Service) (DWD), 46 official weather stations throughout the country recorded temperatures exceeding 40 °C (104 °F) during the height of the heatwave, while 252 stations broke their respective all-time historical records.[^cite_note-WMO23June-237]

German Health Institute RKI reported a cumulated number of heat-related fatalities of 810 in the weeks ending on 21 June.[^cite_note-238] Cities and districts in [North Rhine-Westphalia](38414.md) recorded a high number of fatalities. Cologne recorded four times as many deaths on the weekend of 26 to 28 June compared to an average weekend. Numbers in Remscheid, Wuppertal and Solingen had been twice as high as usual. Emergency rooms had been overcrowded with people having health-related issues like sunstrokes, heat exhaustion and heatstrokes.[^cite_note-239]

#### July

On 30 July, temperatures rose to 40.5 °C. Three states recorded a new July record.[^cite_note-240]

#### Fatalities

By 28 June, the number of heat related fatalities reported by RKI was estimated at 5,120.[^cite_note-RKI-241] In mid-July the German lifeguard federation announced that there had been nearly 100 drowning deaths, most of whom were young men, and 40 of whom were younger than thirty, the most overall since the June 2003 heatwave.[^cite_note-auto2-212]

By 6 August, the number of heat related fatalities reported by RKI was estimated at 11,900. They note that the actual toll may even be much higher. The number of excess fatalities is reported as 14,600.[^cite_note-242]

By 27 August, Germany's [Robert Koch Institute](21272973.md) estimated 15,800 heat-related deaths during summer 2026, the highest total in a decade of monitoring, as repeated heat waves push temperatures above 40 °C (104 °F).[^cite_note-243]

#### Drought

Fears grew on 10 August 2026 that part of the Rhine would become too shallow to safely navigate in future years.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]

### Greece

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

Temperatures reached 42 °C on 18 July 2026.[^cite_note-247]

Temperatures neared 43 °C on 21 July 2026 and 43 °C the following day.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249]

The Hellenic National Meteorological Service (EMY) issued an orange heat warning as 43.5 °C was reached on 22 July 2026[^cite_note-250]

#### Subsequent fires

A forest fire threatened a village on the island of [Paros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paros) on 29 July 2026.[^cite_note-auto9-14] Two firefighters died firefighting in Crete, and one elsewhere in Greece on 29 July 2026.[^cite_note-auto4-13] A forest fire killed two people near [Thessaloniki](40471.md) on 1 July 2026. A forest fire outside Liti, about 15 km/9 miles north-west of the city of Thessaloniki killed two more people on 30 July 2026.[^cite_note-auto8-15]

A Greek and a Dane were killed by forest fire near [Megara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megara) on 2 August.[^cite_note-auto21-12] Another fire hit the tourist island of [Kefalonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kefalonia)[^cite_note-auto21-12] and village of [Psatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psatha?action=edit\&redlink=1), near Athens on the same day.[^cite_note-251]

The Greek fires had mostly been contained by 4 August.[^cite_note-252]

### Hungary

Hungary experienced a heatwave in the second half of June. On 20 June, a temperature of 37.1 °C (98.8 °F) was measured in [Aszód](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asz%C3%B3d). Between 22 and 25 June, temperatures were expected to reach between 30 and 36 °C (86 and 97 °F), whilst the peak of the heatwave was expected to occur between 26 and 28 June, with highs between 32 and 38 °C (90 and 100 °F).[^cite_note-ht-22jun-253]

On 27 June, a temperature of 40.0 °C (104.0 °F) was recorded in [Budakalász](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budakal%C3%A1sz), which marked the first time the value had been reached in Hungary in June, and a temperature of 40.7 °C (105.3 °F) was measured at the same station the following day. Also on 27 June, a temperature of 39.8 °C (103.6 °F) was recorded in the [Újpest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%9Ajpest) district of [Budapest](36787.md), which by far surpassed a 1935 record for the highest temperature measured in the capital on that date.[^cite_note-ht-29jun-254]

On 30 June, a temperature of 42.0 °C (107.6 °F) in [Szécsény](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sz%C3%A9cs%C3%A9ny) set a new all-time temperature record for Hungary, surpassing a July 2007 record of 41.9 °C (107.4 °F) in [Kiskunhalas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiskunhalas).[^cite_note-hvg-30jun-255]

The [Paks Nuclear Power Plant](1835713.md) was shut down because of drought induced record low water levels in the [Danube River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danube_River) on 3 August 2026.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-auto36-41] Concern was raised over the impact river shrinkage and temporary could have on the cooling system of atomic power plants.[^cite_note-auto36-41] It was reactivated on 10 August 2026 as river water began to rise.[^cite_note-auto37-257]

### Iceland

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

There was an unusually high temperature on 31 December 2025, and 1 January 2026.[^cite_note-258]

It was confirmed on 6 July 2026 that Iceland was not affected by the heat as of that date.[^cite_note-259]

### Ireland

**May**

Ireland saw its highest May temperature on record broken on two consecutive days. [Clonmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clonmel) and [Killarney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killarney) recording 28.8 °C (83.8 °F) on 25 May, beating the previous May record of 28.4 °C (83.1 °F) in Kerry in 1997.[^cite_note-260] On 26 May, [Shannon Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shannon_Airport) in [County Clare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_Clare) recorded a temperature of 30.6 °C (87.1 °F).[^cite_note-261] Temperatures at multiple other stations in the country hit 30.9 °C (87.6 °F) on the same day.[^cite_note-auto17-262] This broke the national record for May again. This was also the first time in history a temperature of over 30 °C (86 °F) was recorded in the month of May in Ireland.

On 27 May, temperatures rose above 30.0 °C (86.0 °F) for the second day in a row at Shannon Airport.

[Met Éireann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Met_%C3%89ireann) reported that the temperature in south Ireland was five [standard deviations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_deviation) over the May average.[^cite_note-auto17-262]

**June**

A second heatwave that began building over western Europe on 17 June reached Ireland, with the heat hitting Ireland from the 20 June onwards.

Temperatures of 30 °C (86 °F) across the country were widespread, with that temperature being recorded on multiple days during the week beginning 21 June, reaching a peak on 25 June of 32.2 °C (90.0 °F) in [Athenry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athenry).[^cite_note-263] The heat in June was deemed oppressive as the recorded dew point reached the mid 20s Celsius,[^cite_note-264] meaning that the outside air temperature often felt a lot hotter than recorded. The heat index in [Galway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galway) rose to over 40.0 °C (104.0 °F) on 25 June,[^cite_note-auto18-265] while on multiple days in the same week across the east of Ireland and in cities like [Dublin](8504.md) it reached over 36 °C (97 °F).[^cite_note-auto18-265]

June also saw [tropical nights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_night) recorded in Dublin and [Carlow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlow). On the night of 25 June, temperatures did not fall below 20 °C (68 °F) at [Casement Aerodrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casement_Aerodrome) or Oak Park.

&#x20;\


**July**

After a short reprieve from heat, another high temperature warning was issued by Met Éirean on 7 July. The alert initially lasted for one week, however in parts of the south of the country, it was extended multiple times.&#x20;

On 9 July, heatwave status was reached in [Moorepark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moorepark), [Cork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cork_\(city\))[^cite_note-266] with stations in [Tipperary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tipperary_\(town\)) and [Kilkenny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilkenny) following the next day as temperatures at these locations were above 25 °C (77 °F) for more than five days.&#x20;

On 11 July, [Valentia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentia_Island) Island recorded a temperature of over 30 °C (86 °F), making it the hottest day ever recorded at the station.[^cite_note-267]

The hot spell in July became one of the longest in recorded history in Ireland, with Moorepark recording the second longest heatwave with 13 consecutive days, just one day short of the [1976 record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_heat_wave) of 14.[^cite_note-268]

However, the town of Clonmel provisionally recorded heatwave conditions for 18 consecutive days. If confirmed it would break the previous record.[^cite_note-269]

On the night of 28 July, a tropical night was again recorded in Dublin.&#x20;

**August**

Above average conditions continued into the month of August. Met Éireann issued a fourth yellow weather warning for heat that summer on 12 August, remaining in place until 14 August.&#x20;

The heat was mainly focused on the midlands and eastern half of the country. On 12 August, the Casement Aerodrome station in Dublin recorded a temperature of 31.2 °C (88.2 °F),[^cite_note-270] the highest temperature ever recorded in the month of August for the capital. Temperatures of over 30 °C (86 °F) were also recorded at Oak Park in Carlow, and in other southeastern counties like Wicklow.&#x20;

The heat in August meant that a temperature of over 30 °C (86 °F) had been recorded in four consecutive months in Ireland, (May-August) making 2026 the first year to reach that milestone.&#x20;

Official drought had been declared across the country with a hosepipe ban put in place by Uisce Éireann for six weeks from late July.&#x20;

### Italy

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

Red alert warnings were issued for [Rome](25458.md), [Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence), [Bologna](21069333.md), [Brescia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brescia), and [Turin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turin) on 28 May. Temperatures reached 38 °C (100 °F) in various cities. The thermal stress to the cables in the local grid led to repeated blackouts in Turin.[^cite_note-271]

On 24 June, red alerts were issued for 16 cities, including Rome, [Milan](36511.md), Florence, and Turin.[^cite_note-Krupa-1-195]

On 25 June, courts in [Palermo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palermo) suspended all non-exigent proceedings due to malfunctioning air conditioning, and the newspaper *[Corriere della Sera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corriere_della_Sera)* reported five deaths.[^cite_note-Carrington-6]

On 26 June, the [ANSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agenzia_Nazionale_Stampa_Associata) news agency reported that [Genoa](47332321.md) was the 18th city to be placed under red alert, and the [Uffizi Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uffizi_Gallery) extended a suspension of ticket sales and limited visiting hours.[^cite_note-272] [Clam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clams) fishers on the [Po River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Po_River) estuary reported macro algae growth, which they had to clean off their nets, a blue crab infestation, and a massive die-off of clams in the 21 °C (70 °F) waters. Sea water entered 20 kilometres (12.4 mi) upstream, and the flow of the Po River at large had collapsed from its June average of 1,500 m<sup>3</sup>/s (53,000 cu ft/s) to 300 m<sup>3</sup>/s (11,000 cu ft/s). The environmental group [Legambiente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legambiente) calculated less than three weeks of water reserve.[^cite_note-273]

48.4°C was recorded at [Orani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orani,_Sardinia), Italy.[^cite_note-274]

46.5 °C (115.7 °F) then recorded at Noto, Sicily, Italy on 22/07/2026.[^cite_note-275]

#### Subsequent fires

A large forest fire hit [Tuscany](21967242.md) on May 1, leading to thousands of people being evacuated.[^cite_note-276]

Forest fires occur across Italy on 31/07/2026, with multiple fires in Sardinia, Piedmont and Sicily.[^cite_note-277]

A maximum of 30 °C (86 °F) for one or two days is the criteria is a notable meteorological event with [public health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health) implications; such temperatures for three or more consecutive days is a *kaitra*, an extreme heat event.[^cite_note-284]

### Luxembourg

A level 3 heatwave alert and 16/06/2026 as temperatures were forecast to reach 39 °C.[^cite_note-285] The temperature reached 40 °C on 27 July 2026.[^cite_note-286]

May records were met at several stations run by the Rural Economy and Agriculture Authority on 26 May, though none beat the national May record.[^cite_note-287] On 26 June, 38.6 °C (101.5 °F) was recorded at Findel Airport, setting a new maximum temperature record for June.[^cite_note-Meteolux2026-288] On 27 June, 41.4 °C (106.5 °F) was recorded by the Rural Economy and Agriculture Authority. This was a new all-time record for Luxembourg beating 40.8 °C (105.4 °F) from 2017.[^cite_note-289]

#### Subsequent fires

A major forest fire hit the [Herceg Novi municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herceg_Novi_municipality) on 29 June 2026.[^cite_note-296]

### Moldova

Moldova experienced "extreme heat" in late June. Emergency shelters were set up by the [General Inspectorate for Emergency Situations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_\(Moldova\)) on 28 June, with 3,567 people receiving assistance there on 28 and 29 June. Rescue workers also distributed 1,133 litres of water.[^cite_note-auto1-297]

2,447 people sought assistance in shelters on 29 June in [Chișinău](56635.md) and across various districts, such as [Anenii Noi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anenii_Noi_District), [Cahul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cahul_District), [Ștefan Vodă](3077636.md), [Cantemir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantemir_District), [Ialoveni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ialoveni_District), [Căușeni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83u%C8%99eni_District), [Hîncești](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%AEnce%C8%99ti_District) , [Leova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leova_District), [Taraclia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taraclia_District) and [Cimișlia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cimi%C8%99lia_District). The shelters were in Chișinău on 30 June, in the sectors of [Botanica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectorul_Botanica), [Rîșcani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectorul_R%C3%AE%C8%99cani), [Buiucani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectorul_Buiucani), [Ciocana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectorul_Ciocana) and [Centru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectorul_Centru).[^cite_note-auto1-297]

Emergency medical teams responded to 19 heat-related emergencies between 22 and 28 June. Thirteen people suffered heatstroke, and six cases of [syncope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncope_\(medicine\)) were recorded. A yellow heat warning was issued for southern Moldova on 22 June. On 30 June, the same heat warning was issued for northern Moldova, with an orange warning in effect for central Moldova and a red warning in effect for southern Moldova that day.[^cite_note-auto-298]

Villages in Moldova were deserted by noon, and most activities, including farming, had to be done in the morning. Traders and shopkeepers reported a record sale of ice cream, mainly purchased in the evening, when temperatures were lower.[^cite_note-299]

A yellow heatwave warning was issued in Moldova on 4 July, with temperatures reaching 35 °C (95 °F) in the south.[^cite_note-300]

### Netherlands

In the Netherlands, a heatwave is defined as five consecutive summer days of at least 25 °C (77 °F), of which at least three reach tropical-level heat (30 °C (86 °F)).[^cite_note-301] During such occasions, the National Heatwave Plan is activated by Dutch public health institute [RIVM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_for_Public_Health_and_the_Environment),[^cite_note-302] [ProRail](168969.md) and the [NS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nederlandse_Spoorwegen) runs fewer trains as a precautionary measure.[^cite_note-303] The heat also necessitated water spraying to keep the drawbridges across the canals functioning,[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] as well as preventing [road surfaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_surface) from [thermal buckling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_expansion).[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] "Winter" salt trucks were also deployed for increasing [albedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albedo) and lowering the risk of [asphalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asphalt_concrete) softening.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310]

#### May

On 26 May, the temperature in [Ell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ell,_Netherlands) reached 30.7 °C (87.3 °F), becoming the hottest ever on that calendar day.[^cite_note-311] This was also the third consecutive day with tropical-level heat, thus it classified as the first regional heatwave of 2026.[^cite_note-312]

#### June

The first national heatwave, or the second regional heatwave, began on 18 June, when the temperature in [De Bilt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Bilt) reached 29.6 °C (85.3 °F). A day later, the temperature in De Bilt rose to 33.4 °C (92.1 °F), marking the first tropical day. After several summer days, the second tropical day followed on 24 June with 34 °C (93 °F). As of 29 June, data concluded this as the longest-ever-recorded consecutive heatwave in the Netherlands, lasting 11 days until 1 July.[^cite_note-313] This is also the longest regional "super-heatwave" which lasted six days.[^cite_note-314]

#### July

The third regional heatwave began on 13 July. In places like [Gilze en Rijen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilze_en_Rijen), temperatures crossed the 25.4 °C (77.7 °F) threshold, fulfilling the 5-day consecutive warmup requirements for KNMI classification. Further south and east, in [Volkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkel), [Horst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horst,_Limburg), [Ell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ell,_Netherlands), and [Maastricht](20125.md), the mercury surged past 30 °C (86 °F).[^cite_note-315] The RIVM national heat plan was also reactivated.[^cite_note-316]

#### August

A fourth regional heatwave began on 28 July and ended on 06 August.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] The KNMI issued a yellow warning for the entire country, as temperatures up to 36 °C (97 °F) were forecast.[^cite_note-319] The fifth regional heatwave began on 8 August and is as of 14 August still ongoing, equalling the 1947 record of five regional heatwaves in one year.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-\:6-321] The KNMI issued a yellow warning for the entire country, as temperatures up to 38 °C (100 °F) were forecast.[^cite_note-\:6-321]. The ongoing heat in August also broke the 1947 all-time regional record for the most amount of tropical days, with the record currently standing on 31 as of 12 August.[^cite_note-322]

#### Record-breaking statistics

| Date | New record | Previous record year | Old record |

| 26 May | 30.7 °C (87.3 °F) | 2005 | 29.1 °C (84.4 °F) |
| 24 June[^cite_note-Sandelowsky-324] | 33.7 °C (92.7 °F) | 32.6 °C (90.7 °F) |  |

| New record | Date | Location | Previous record | Location | Old record |
