24 June is the sixth-ever recorded day with tropical conditions (above 30 °C (86 °F)) throughout the country, as well as the first-ever one for June.[^cite_note-Sandelowsky-324] The night of 24 June was the hottest ever recorded in that month of the year.[^cite_note-nos.nl-2-327]

| Date | Code level | Location | Reason/Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 22–25 June | Yellow | Northern Provinces; [Wadden Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frisian_Islands) | Risk for localised heat weather hazard[^cite_note-knmi-1-328] |
| Orange | Rest of the country | Expected temperature up to 36 °C (97 °F)[^cite_note-329] |  |
| 26–27 June | Northern Provinces; Wadden Islands; [Zeeland](52229.md) | High chance for heat weather hazard[^cite_note-knmi-1-328] |  |
| Red | Rest of the country | Expected temperature up to 40 °C (104 °F)[^cite_note-knmi-2-330] |  |
| Upgraded from preemptive code orange |  |  |  |
| First-ever code red alert for heat[^cite_note-knmi-2-330] |  |  |  |
| Except [Gelderland](57160.md), [Limburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limburg_\(Netherlands\)), [North Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Brabant), and [Overijssel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overijssel), the rest of the country was downgraded to orange on 27 June |  |  |  |
| 10–15 July | Yellow | [South Holland](28687.md); [Utrecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utrecht_\(province\)); Gelderland; Overijssel; Zeeland; Noord Brabant; Limburg | Expected temperature above 25 °C (77 °F)[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] |
| 12-16 August | Yellow | All provinces, including Wadden Islands | Expected temperature up to 38 ºC (100 ºF)[^cite_note-\:6-321] |

On the afternoon of 25 June, the preemptive nationwide code orange of 26 June was upgraded to code red except for the north, Wadden Islands, and Zeeland. The code red alert was extended to 27 June when data suggests that the [heat dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_dome) shifted later than expected.[^cite_note-333]

On 26 June, KNMI issued level 10 on the heat force scale,[^cite_note-334] the fourth time in De Bilt since the scale's establishment in 1991.[^cite_note-knmi-2-330] This day also classed as the first-ever "super-heatwave" in the Netherlands, wherein the temperature stayed above 35 °C (95 °F) for three consecutive days.[^cite_note-335] Throughout the week, schools operated on a special heatwave schedule, and on 26 June most of the primary schools and also secondary schools closed due to the extreme heat.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] The red alert has led to the cancellation of various events, including the Dutch U18 and U20 athletics championships,[^cite_note-338] and the [Defqon.1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defqon.1_Festival) music festival in [Biddinghuizen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biddinghuizen).[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] On 27 June, numerous events were also were temporarily suspended or ended early as a number of [safety regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_region) declared it unsafe to host any forms of outdoor mass gathering.[^cite_note-nos.nl-1-341]

The all-time record of June dated back to 1947, when it reached 37.9 °C (100.2 °F) in [Venlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venlo) on 27 June, it was broken by 39.4 °C (102.9 °F) in Ell on the afternoon of 26 June 2026.[^cite_note-knmi-2-330] Previously, on 25 July [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_European_heatwaves), the absolute all-time heat record from 1944 was broken by 40.7 °C (105.3 °F) in [Gilze-Rijen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilze-Rijen_Air_Base).[^cite_note-342]

#### Severe thunderstorms

##### 19–20 June

On the night of 19 June, the [frontal system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weather_front) of the approaching heatwave generated a severe thunderstorm that tore through the entire [Benelux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benelux) area. The Netherlands recorded over 188,307 lightning strikes, breaking the all-time daily record.[^cite_note-343] Significant storm damage took place in eastern [Groningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groningen_\(province\)).[^cite_note-344] A wedding with 150 guests in Twente was flooded during the ceremony, flash flood occurred after a [river bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_\(geography\)) burst.[^cite_note-345] A 25-year-old woman died when a tree fell on her moving car near [Coevorden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coevorden).[^cite_note-346] An [NL-Alert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NL-Alert) was issued the following morning for the overwhelmed emergency service due to the widespread response to the damages to houses and trees.[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349]

##### 27–28 June

A code orange alert was issued for severe thunderstorm from the afternoon of 27 June to the early morning of 28 June, worse than the night of 19 June due to higher amount of [atmospheric energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atmospheric_electricity).[^cite_note-350] Monitors recorded 413,860 lightnings, breaking the record that just set a week ago by more than double.[^cite_note-Onweerarchief2-351] Various regions across the Netherlands were hit with [hailstones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hail) up to 5 centimetres (2.0 in).[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-nos.nl-1-341] Insurers received many damage reports due to hail and lightning strikes, particularly from the regions around [Amsterdam](844.md) and [Utrecht](50333.md) and from [Flevoland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flevoland).[^cite_note-353]

Train traffic experienced various disruptions due to lightning strikes and trees and branches on the tracks. In [Gouda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gouda,_South_Holland), a lightning strike caused serious technical problems, halting all operations between Gouda, [The Hague](30269.md), and [Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Centraal_station). A [tram](30733.md) in [Scheveningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheveningen) was hit by a lightning strike and stalled in the middle of an [intersection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersection_\(road\)).[^cite_note-354]

Lightning strikes also caused several fires in Amsterdam, [Wassenaar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wassenaar), [Geldrop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geldrop), [Snelrewaard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snelrewaard), [Helmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmond), [Landsmeer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landsmeer), [Nunspeet](118627.md), and [Beek en Donk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beek_en_Donk), among others. A historic barn at Oldenoordweg in Toornwerd, Groningen caught fire from the lightning strike.[^cite_note-355] In [Veldhoven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veldhoven), the historic 1858 [windmill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windmill) of [Zilster Molen](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zilster%20molen) was torn apart by a strong [wind gust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_gust), it collapsed onto nearby houses and cars.[^cite_note-356]

Safety regions received an overwhelming amount of damage reports, multiple control rooms were near-capacity. For instance, the Midden- and West-Brabant safety region was so busy that an extra control room was set up at a fire station.[^cite_note-357] People in that region were urged to call the emergency services only in cases of immediate danger, so that the control room remained available for urgent situations.[^cite_note-358] Reports of [flash flooding](244391.md) also surged across Dutch cities.[^cite_note-nos.nl-1-341]

This storm, generated by the front with extreme temperature difference along retreating [high-pressure area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-pressure_area), marked the end of the June heatwave.[^cite_note-knmi-2-330]

#### Other heat-related disruptions

Overall, during the heatwaves, six deaths were caused by accidents during water activities.[^cite_note-NethMulti-359] [RIVM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_for_Public_Health_and_the_Environment) attributed approximately 480 excess deaths to heat, which is mostly the elderly population or people with pre-existing health conditions, in the eastern and southern parts of the country.[^cite_note-360] The death toll is expected to rise as reports are still being processed in the coming weeks.[^cite_note-361]

During the June heatwave, there were numerous reports of drawbridge malfunctions across the Netherlands.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] Many municipalities halted all bridge operations, stranding ship traffic.[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] The railroads also suffered vehicle and infrastructure failures.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-treinreiziger.nl-369][^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] Various roads across the Netherlands also suffered [structural failure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Structural_integrity_and_failure) due to extreme thermal conditions on 26 and 27 June, necessitating emergency repairs.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] Dutch energy company [Essent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essent) reported that residential electricity consumption shot up by 50% during the 11-day-long extreme heat.[^cite_note-376]

The Dutch government declared a drought on 3 August, water levels are the lowest since 1947.[^cite_note-377] On 3 September the national water shortage is officially over, but landscape recovery measures will carry on for the coming months. [^cite_note-378]

### North Macedonia

The temperature reached around 35 °C in Skopje on 17 July 2026.[^cite_note-386]

### Poland

Given low May rainfall, Poland faced considerably increased risk of [wildfires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildfires) in its forests and [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks_of_Poland) as the [Omega block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_block) moved east.[^cite_note-387] Several municipalities set up [water curtains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_curtain) for the public, and [Polish State Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_State_Railways) warning of deformed tracks and sagging power lines, offered full refunds for booked weekend travel.[^cite_note-388] On 28 June, 40.5 °C (104.9 °F) was recorded in [Słubice](656704.md). This is the highest temperature ever recorded in Poland,[^cite_note-TVN-389] the previous record being 40.2 °C (104.4 °F) in 1921.[^cite_note-lemonde-1-206]

On 28 June, it was reported that a woman had died in [Chełm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Che%C5%82m) due to high temperatures.[^cite_note-390] On the same day, during an edition of the LOTTO PolandBike Marathon held in [Marki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marki), two race participants, aged 30 and 71, died after failing to reach the finish line. The body of the first cyclist was discovered later that day a short distance from the race route. The second was found on 29 June following an extensive search involving police, fire brigades, search and rescue teams and drones. Initial reports suggested that the extreme heat, with temperatures reaching nearly 40 °C (104 °F), was a possible contributing factor. The exact circumstances remain the subject of investigation.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392] A 60-year-old man died in a hospital in Konin after suffering from overheating while working on a farm.[^cite_note-393] A 47-year-old woman died while sunbathing in the yard of her home. Overheating is suspected to be the cause of death; this will be investigated.[^cite_note-394]

On 29 June, in [Sanok](390560.md), a 52-year-old man died at work.[^cite_note-395] At the same time, a 90-year-old man died in the hospital in [Zielona Góra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zielona_G%C3%B3ra). The cause of death was likely overheating; doctors recorded a body temperature of 43 degrees Celsius.[^cite_note-396]

### Portugal

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

Portugal faced the threat of 42 °C (108 °F) high temperatures with yellow weather warnings issued initially only in the districts of [Leiria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leiria_District), [Lisbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisbon_District), [Santarém](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santar%C3%A9m_District), [Setúbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Set%C3%BAbal_District), [Portalegre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portalegre_District), [Évora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89vora_District), and [Beja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beja_District), before being expanded to all districts in Portugal due to significant public health concern and "very high" wildfire risks.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399] Continental Portugal experienced nearly nationwide [tropical nights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_nights), meaning that the daily low temperature was above 20 °C (68 °F) from the [Alentejo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alentejo) to the [Algarve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algarve).[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] [Mora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mora,_Portugal) recorded a temperature of 40.3 °C (104.5 °F) on 27 May, setting the hottest May record on nationally. In addition, 22 stations recorded the highest maximum-temperature on record, with one on 26 May and the rest on 27 May.[^cite_note-port1-403] Temperatures then hit 44 °C on June 1.[^cite_note-404]

#### Subsequent fires

A large forest fire hit the municipality of Valpaços, Vila Real on 29 July. [^cite_note-405]

Over 14,000 hectares forest burn down in Portugal during July, with the North region hardest hit[^cite_note-406]

### Romania

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

#### Late May Heatwave

On 27 May, 32.8 °C (91.0 °F) was recorded in [Oravița](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oravi%C8%9Ba).

#### Late June Heatwave

On 28 June, [Administrația Națională de Meteorologie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administra%C8%9Bia_Na%C8%9Bional%C4%83_de_Meteorologie) (ANM) issued Code Red Heat Warnings for 16 counties. Temperatures hit 39.8 °C (103.6 °F) in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni). On 29 June, the Code Red Heat Warnings was extended to 35 counties, including the capital city of [Bucharest](36877.md). Temperatures hit 40.6 °C (105.1 °F) in [Jimbolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimbolia) and Săcueni. On 30 June, the Red Heat Warnings have been extended to 39 counties. Temperatures hit 40.8 °C (105.4 °F) in Jimbolia and Săcueni. 3 people died due to the heat, including a 56-year-old shepherd from [Iași](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ia%C8%99i) due to prolonged exposure from the sun.[^cite_note-Antena3RO-16][^cite_note-407]

The heatwave triggered intense and deadly thunderstorms. One person died due to the thunderstorms in [Găneasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C4%83neasa,_Ilfov), [Ilfov County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilfov_County) when a tree crushed a car.

#### Mid July Heatwave

On 16 July, [Administrația Națională de Meteorologie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administra%C8%9Bia_Na%C8%9Bional%C4%83_de_Meteorologie) (ANM) issued Code Yellow Heat Warnings for 4 counties. 36.8 °C (98.2 °F) was recorded in [Sânnicolau Mare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A2nnicolau_Mare). On 18 July, the Code Yellow Heat Warnings were expanded to 7 counties. 37.4 °C (99.3 °F) was recorded in [Calafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calafat). On 19 July, the Code Yellow Heat Warnings were expanded to 15 counties. Temperatures reached 36.9 °C (98.4 °F) in [Calafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calafat). On 20 July, the Code Yellow Heat Warnings were reduced to 10 counties.

#### Late July/Early August Heatwave

On 30 July, 37.0 °C (98.6 °F) was recorded in [Arad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arad,_Romania). On 31 July 38.3 °C (100.9 °F) was recorded in [Sânnicolau Mare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A2nnicolau_Mare). On 1 August, [Administrația Națională de Meteorologie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administra%C8%9Bia_Na%C8%9Bional%C4%83_de_Meteorologie) (ANM) issued Code Red Heat Warnings for [Arad County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arad_County), [Bihor County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bihor_County) and [Satu Mare County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satu_Mare_County). 39.7 °C (103.5 °F) was recorded in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni). On 3 August, the Code Red Heat Warning was expanded to 4 counties, with the [Timiș County](269327.md) joining the Code Red Heat Warning. 39.5 °C (103.1 °F) was recorded in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni). On 4 August, the Code Orange Heat Warnings and the Code Yellow Heat Warnings were expanded to the whole country. 39.5 °C (103.1 °F) was recorded in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni), again. On 5 August, the Code Red Heat Warnings were expanded to 7 counties. [Caraș-Severin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cara%C8%99-Severin_County), [Maramureș County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maramure%C8%99_County) and [Sălaj County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83laj_County) joined the Code Red Heat Warning. Over three quarters of the country joined the Code Orange Heat Warnings, while the rest of the country were in Code Yellow Heat Warnings. 40.5 °C (104.9 °F) was recorded in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni). On 6 August, 41.2 °C (106.2 °F) was recorded in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni).

On 11 August, 39.8 °C (103.6 °F) was recorded in [Săcueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83cueni).&#x20;

On 20 August, 39.0 °C (102.2 °F) was recorded in [Sânnicolau Mare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A2nnicolau_Mare). On 21 August, 40.6 °C (105.1 °F) was recorded in [Moldova Veche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moldova_Veche).

Serbia's Republic Hydrometeorological Institute (RHMZ) reported on 27 June 2025 that the nation's highest temperatures had just occurred at:[^cite_note-auto3-413]

- Cuprija and Zajecar- 40 °C.

  [^cite_note-auto3-413]

- Kikinda, Negotin and Leskovac- 39 °C.

  [^cite_note-auto3-413]

- Belgrade- 38 °C.

  [^cite_note-auto3-413]

The heat hit 35 C (95 F) Belgrade 12 July.[^cite_note-auto14-414]

Serbia’s the [Pančevo refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan%C4%8Devo_refinery), the only oil refinery in the country, cut production by 50% as the Danube shrank to near unnavigable level on 4 August 2026.[^cite_note-415]

### Slovakia

From mid-June 2026, the Slovak Hydrometeorological Institute (SHMÚ) issued repeated high-temperature warnings as the second European heatwave of the season reached Slovakia.[^cite_note-416] First- and second-level alerts were progressively expanded across the Bratislava, Trnava and Nitra Regions and southern districts elsewhere, with third-level warnings later covering large parts of the south-west ahead of the peak.[^cite_note-417] On the night of 27 June the temperature at Bratislava–Koliba fell no lower than 26.3 °C (79.3 °F), the highest June minimum in the SHMÚ network since at least 1951; further exceptionally warm nights followed.[^cite_note-418]

By late June around 37–44 % of Slovakia was experiencing significant to extreme soil drought. SHMÚ warned of high wildfire risk and the Public Health Authority of the Slovak Republic issued advisories for vulnerable groups, including children, the elderly, pregnant women and the chronically ill. Emergency services recorded hundreds of heat-related collapses; rail lines suffered speed restrictions and track buckling; and animal-welfare incidents were reported.[^cite_note-419] On 29 June a provisional national record of 41.0 °C (105.8 °F) was measured at the precipitation station in [Turňa nad Bodvou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tur%C5%88a_nad_Bodvou), with 40.5–40.6 °C recorded at several other stations (including Mužla); an IT-system outage at SHMÚ temporarily disrupted real-time data that day.[^cite_note-420] The following day, 30 June, 41.3 °C (106.3 °F) was recorded at the automatic station in [Kamenica nad Hronom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamenica_nad_Hronom). After verification SHMÚ experts formally confirmed the value as the new all-time national record on 9–10 July 2026.[^cite_note-421]

After a cooler period, a second heatwave developed from late July into early August 2026. On 1 August temperatures again reached 40 °C (104 °F) at [Štrkovec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0trkovec) in the [Rimavská Sobota District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rimavsk%C3%A1_Sobota_District)—the second occasion in a single summer (in two distinct episodes) that the 40 °C threshold had been exceeded, an occurrence unprecedented in the modern observational record. Third-degree heat warnings covered southern regions, with highs oscillating around 40 °C expected to persist into the following working week; drought conditions remained severe in the south and emergency services again recorded dozens of heat-related collapses.[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] On 6 August the meteorological station of [Dolné Plachtince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doln%C3%A9_Plachtince) had unexpectedly recorded a maximum temperature of 42.2 °C (108.0 °F) , surpassing the new national record from the day before of 41.3 °C (106.3 °F). This record was not yet verified by SHMÚ.[^cite_note-424]

### Slovenia

#### May and June

Between 25 and 27 May, the [Slovenian Environment Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovenian_Environment_Agency) (ARSO) recorded temperatures above 30 °C (86 °F) at several stations in its network, reaching as high as 33.5 °C (92.3 °F) at [Cerklje ob Krki Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerklje_ob_Krki_Airport) on 27 May.[^cite_note-arso-1-425]

According to ARSO's definition, a heatwave in Slovenia is a period of time when there is a minimum of three consecutive days with average temperatures of at least 24 °C (75 °F) in its central and eastern parts or 25 °C (77 °F) in its western parts, respectively. Therefore, the first heatwave to affect the country in 2026 began on 18 June, when the agency announced that it expected highs above 30 °C (86 °F) to persist for at least 12 days.[^cite_note-delo-18jun-426] On 24 June, ARSO reported that the peak of the heatwave was likely to occur on 28 or 29 June.[^cite_note-slo-24jun-427] On 27 June, the agency recorded a temperature of 37.4 °C (99.3 °F) in [Dolenje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolenje,_Ajdov%C5%A1%C4%8Dina), followed by 37.2 °C (99.0 °F) in [Podnanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podnanos), as temperatures rose above 33 °C (91 °F) in almost all lowland areas of Slovenia.[^cite_note-slo-27jun-428] Between 27 and 30 June, the highest heat warning issued by ARSO was in effect for most of the country's southwestern areas, whilst the agency's second-highest heat warning was in effect for the rest of Slovenia.[^cite_note-slo-24jun-427][^cite_note-slo-27jun-428]

On 28 June, temperatures exceeded 36 °C (97 °F) at several ARSO stations in southwestern and central parts of the country; a temperature of 37.5 °C (99.5 °F) was recorded in Podnanos, the highest value measured during the heatwave up to that point, and a temperature of 36.9 °C (98.4 °F) at the agency's station in the [Bežigrad District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be%C5%BEigrad_District) of [Ljubljana](42374.md) set a new June temperature record for the capital. On 29 June, eleven ARSO stations measured highs of at least 37.0 °C (98.6 °F), which included a temperature of 38.7 °C (101.7 °F) in Podnanos and 38.2 °C (100.8 °F) in [Bilje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilje,_Miren-Kostanjevica), respectively. The former set a new June temperature record for Slovenia, surpassing a 2025 record of 38.4 °C (101.1 °F) in [Dobliče](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dobli%C4%8De) and marking the first time since a previous 2022 record of 38.0 °C (100.4 °F) that the value had been reached in Podnanos in June.[^cite_note-slo-29jun-429][^cite_note-arso-2-430][^cite_note-431]

Hot weather continued on 30 June and 1 July, with temperatures in Podnanos reaching 38.1 °C (100.6 °F) and 36.3 °C (97.3 °F), respectively, as several ARSO stations in the [Vipava Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vipava_Valley) measured highs in excess of 34 °C (93 °F) on every day between 18 June and 1 July. Between 28 and 30 June, 20 stations in the agency's network registered highs of at least 36.0 °C (96.8 °F), with 12 of the 20 stations setting new June temperature records for their respective locations.[^cite_note-arso-2-430] During the afternoon of 30 June, thunderstorms caused by the heat affected southwestern and central Slovenia, before occurring in various parts of the country the following day, which marked the end of the heatwave.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433]

#### July and August

A second heatwave affected Slovenia during the week from 13 to 19 July, but it was less intense and shorter than the first one. Afternoon highs did not exceed 35 °C (95 °F) and thunderstorms were frequent. Because most precipitation since mid-June had been in the form of short-lived, local thunderstorms and downpours, drought was felt in most regions of the country and there was a heightened risk of wildfires for several days.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435]

The third heatwave to affect Slovenia in 2026 began on 29 July and was initially expected to last at least one week, with its peak likely to occur on 31 July or 1 August, when temperatures were predicted to exceed 35 °C (95 °F) in all lowland areas of the country and reach approximately 39 °C (102 °F) in the Vipava Valley and parts of western Slovenia, as drought and a heightened risk of wildfires persisted in most regions of the country.[^cite_note-436] ARSO later reported that the heatwave was likely to continue until the end of the first week of August and could lead to some places surpassing a 2015 record of 10 consecutive days with highs of at least 35 °C (95 °F).[^cite_note-437]

On 31 July, ARSO measured July temperature records at 28 stations in its network, with 15 of the 28 stations registering the highest temperatures for their respective locations since record-keeping began. The highest temperature of the day was measured in Dolenje near [Ajdovščina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajdov%C5%A1%C4%8Dina) at 40.0 °C (104.0 °F), a new temperature record for the location and the first time the value had been reached in Slovenia in 2026. It was followed by a temperature of 39.6 °C (103.3 °F) in Podnanos.[^cite_note-arsox-31jul-438][^cite_note-439] On 1 August, ARSO measured a temperature of 39.2 °C (102.6 °F) in Bilje, followed by 39.1 °C (102.4 °F) in [Škocjan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0kocjan,_Diva%C4%8Da) and 38.8 °C (101.8 °F) in Dolenje, with all three locations surpassing their respective August temperature records for the first time since 2017.[^cite_note-440]

On 2 August, temperatures at the ARSO station in [Vedrijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedrijan) did not fall below 28.7 °C (83.7 °F), which set a new national record for the highest daily low.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-rtvslo-3aug-442] Around noon that day, a temperature of 40.1 °C (104.2 °F) was measured at the agency's station in the Markovec area of [Koper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koper), surpassing a 2017 record of 38.8 °C (101.8 °F) for the location, after the [bora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bora_\(wind\)) that had started overnight continued and contributed to an excessively hot morning in the region.[^cite_note-rtvslo-3aug-442][^cite_note-arso-3-443] Eventually, ARSO registered an average daily temperature of 32.3 °C (90.1 °F) at the station for 2 August, which was the highest value among the three stations in its network that registered an average daily temperature in excess of 30 °C (86 °F) that day, the other two being Vedrijan and Bilje.[^cite_note-444]

On 5 August, temperatures in Ljubljana reached at least 35 °C (95 °F) for the eighth consecutive day, surpassing a 2013 record of seven consecutive days with such highs measured in the capital. Late in the afternoon, the heat caused the formation of heavy thunderstorms in parts of western Slovenia.[^cite_note-delo-5aug-445][^cite_note-446] Several places at an elevation above 1,000 m (3,300 ft) were also affected by the heatwave, with the ARSO station at [Rogla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rogla), located at 1,495 m (4,905 ft) above sea level, registering a daily low of 21 °C (70 °F) between 3 and 4 August to become the highest station in the agency's network to experience a "tropical night" in 2026.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-delo-5aug-445]

#### Subsequent fires

On 31 July, it was reported that dry terrain and possibly a lightning strike had caused the spread of a forest fire in the [Municipality of Bovec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Bovec), which originally started as an underground fire three days earlier, prompting an all-day firefighting operation that also required a temporary closure of the [Mangart Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangart_Saddle). Approximately 12 ha (30 acres) of forested landscape were affected.[^cite_note-448] On 3 August, it was reported that the forest fire was under control, even though underground fires still required firefighters to monitor the affected area.[^cite_note-449]

On 7 August, a forest fire broke out due to a suspected lightning strike in a remote mountainous area near [Kranjska Gora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kranjska_Gora), requiring an aerial firefighting operation, as the affected area was not accessible by road.[^cite_note-450] Although the fire had been contained by 8 August, it reignited and started spreading again on 10 August, requiring the aerial firefighting operation to resume.[^cite_note-451]

### Spain

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

Temperatures in Spain began rising on 19 May. By 28 May, [Santander Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santander_Airport) recorded six days with temperatures higher than 30 °C (86 °F). Before then, only two days prior to June had recorded such temperatures. In addition, [Badajoz Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badajoz_Airport) had temperatures over 38 °C (100 °F) for the first time in May. Temperatures across the southwestern quadrant of Spain recorded highs of 37–39 °C (99–102 °F).[^cite_note-452] [Vinebre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinebre) recorded a temperature of 39.5 °C (103.1 °F), setting the hottest May temperature ever recorded in [Catalonia](6822.md).[^cite_note-453] The [Health Ministry](33528060.md) recorded 101 heat-related deaths in May, the highest ever recorded for the month of May since records began in 2015.[^cite_note-454]

On 22 June, temperatures peaked at 45.1 °C (113.2 °F) in [Andújar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/And%C3%BAjar), southern Spain.[^cite_note-455] Between 21 and 24 June, 108 fatalities were recorded.[^cite_note-456] On 25 June, the Daily Mortality Monitoring System (stylized MoMo)[^cite_note-457] statistically calculated that 212 deaths were linked to the heatwave.[^cite_note-Krupa-2-196] By 26 June, 327 heat-related deaths had been registered since 21 June.[^cite_note-Ahmed-92] The [Carlos III Health Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_III_Health_Institute) reported at least 1,028 heat-related deaths occurred during the heatwave in June.[^cite_note-Spain1k-458]

[Barcelona](4443.md) recorded 40.5 °C (104.9 °F) on 8 July, setting the highest maximum temperature since records began.[^cite_note-459] A [wildfire](83658163.md) broke out on 9 July in southeastern [Almería province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Almer%C3%ADa), killing at least 12 and injuring at least eight, while 23 others are missing.[^cite_note-460]

#### Subsequent fires

Antonio Sanz, Andalusia's health and emergencies minister, said on 11 July 2026 that a fire had burned it's way across 6,600 hectares (16,300 acres) of Andalusia's land.[^cite_note-461]

A major forest fire at Navaluenga, Ávila, on 22 July[^cite_note-auto31-462]

Bush fires threatened Madrid on 24 July.[^cite_note-auto5-463]

The arsonist who started the major forest fire at Navaluenga, Ávila, on 22 July; was jailed in 30 July[^cite_note-auto31-462]

Spain's prime minister, Pedro Sánchez, raised concerns about climate change after visiting a small town in the southwestern region of Extremadura on 2 August.[^cite_note-auto13-464] A man in Madrid died of heat stroke the same day.[^cite_note-auto13-464]

Three fires broke out in [Huelva province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huelva_province) in southwestern Spain between 6 and 7 August and the fire hit the Doñana National Park on 7 August.[^cite_note-auto31-462]

Firefighters battled with major wildfires in [Castellón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Castell%C3%B3n) on 9 August 2026[^cite_note-465] and [Andalusia](2736.md) on 10 August 2026.[^cite_note-466]

### Sweden

Orange warnings were issued for southern Sweden from 25 June onwards as heatwaves moved their way northward from the rest of the continent.[^cite_note-467] Temperatures exceeded 30 degrees for the first time on that day in Ronneby-Bredåkra with 30.6 °C (87.1 °F)[^cite_note-468] and have been forecasted to reach up to 35 °C (95 °F) until 29 June.[^cite_note-469] On 25 June, a 600 m-long freight train derailed on the main route between [Göteborg and Borås](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coast-to-Coast_Line) near [Bollebygd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollebygd) because of heat-induced track warping. No one was injured in the accident, and as of 27 June 2026, there is no estimate of when the track will reopen.[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471]

### Switzerland

The unusually early heatwave set new daily highs in many stations in the latter part of May.[^cite_note-swissinfo-472] The 0 °C (32 °F) [isotherm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isotherm_\(contour_line\)), which is to say the [freezing level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freezing_level), rose to 4,328 m (14,199 ft) on 25 May, the third highest recorded in May.[^cite_note-473] [Biasca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biasca) recorded 34.8 °C (94.6 °F) on 28 May, just 0.3 °C (0.54 °F) below the May national record and the highest May temperature ever recorded on the southern side of the [Alps](981.md).[^cite_note-swissinfo-472] Overall, the month of May was 1.8 °C (3.2 °F) above average and the fourth hottest on record.[^cite_note-swissinfo-472][^cite_note-474]

On 25 June, [MétéoSuisse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A9t%C3%A9oSuisse) announced they had exceeded 37 °C (99 °F) in June for the first time since modern records began in 1946, with 38.0 °C (100.4 °F) in [Basel](4911.md).[^cite_note-Carrington-6] This record was broken the following day, 26 June, with 38.8 °C (101.8 °F) in [Binningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binningen,_Switzerland).[^cite_note-475] And on the 27th, Glacier Monitoring (GLAMOS) announced that the second-earliest melt-off of the winter's accumulated ice and snow was expected on Monday. Its records began in 2000, and the earliest previous melt-off was marked on 26 June 2022.[^cite_note-Ali-175]

### Turkey

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_European_heatwaves\&action=edit\&section=). *(July 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

A major temperature increase hits cities across Turkey, including Antalya and [Istanbul](3391396.md) on 21 June 2026.[^cite_note-476]

#### Subsequent fires

A bush fire closed the main road between [Fethiye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethiye) and [Antalya](361527.md) and a hospital was evacuated because of a bush fire in Muğla province on 29 July 2026.[^cite_note-auto4-13]

Hundreds are Antalya's [Kaş district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ka%C5%9F_district?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [Muğla province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mu%C4%9Fla_province) due to near by wid fires on 30 July 2026.[^cite_note-477]

### Ukraine

As the heat dome shifted eastward into Central and Eastern Europe in late June, Ukraine experienced "intense heat" with temperatures between 35 and 38 °C (95 and 100 °F), which was warned by the [Ukrainian Hydrometeorological Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Hydrometeorological_Center) on 29 June. Residents cooled off in the river, which can be seen en masse in [Kyiv](585629.md) at the beaches of the [Dnieper River](380138.md).[^cite_note-478]

The ongoing [Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md) resulted in significant damage to Ukraine's energy infrastructure, the stress on the electricity grid was exacerbated by the heat. [Energy rationing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_rationing) and [rolling blackouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_blackout) were implemented by the main [operators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_utility) across several regions on 30 June.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481][^cite_note-482]

The highest daytime temperatures during the heatwave were concentrated across western and southern Ukraine, culminating on 28 June when [Lutsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutsk) recorded the country's peak temperature of 36.7 °C (98.1 °F). In [Lviv Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lviv_Oblast), temperatures climbed from 30.2 °C (86.4 °F) on 25 June to 31.7 °C (89.1 °F) on 26 June, 32.4 °C (90.3 °F) on 27 June, and 35.8 °C (96.4 °F) on 28 June. Similarly, [Zakarpattia Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zakarpattia_Oblast) also saw consecutive record-breaking days, rising 33.4 °C (92.1 °F) on 26 June to 34.6 °C (94.3 °F) on 27 June, and 36.4 °C (97.5 °F) on 28 June. Other western regions also registered record highs, including [Ternopil Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ternopil_Oblast) at 35.5 °C (95.9 °F), [Khmelnytskyi Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmelnytskyi_Oblast) at 32.8 °C (91.0 °F), and [Rivne Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivne_Oblast) at 36 °C (97 °F) on 28 June. Record temperatures were established across several oblasts, including [Chernivtsi Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chernivtsi_Oblast) at 35.9 °C (96.6 °F), [Vinnytsia Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinnytsia_Oblast) at 34.6 °C (94.3 °F), and [Zhytomyr Oblast](1567596.md) at 33 °C (91 °F). The heat peaked on 28 June, with [Kirovohrad Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirovohrad_Oblast) hitting 35 °C (95 °F) and [Odesa Oblast](61438266.md) hitting 36.2 °C (97.2 °F).[^cite_note-483]

On 6 August, temperatures up to 39 °C (102 °F) were recorded in Kyiv and Rivne, with the highest being 40.2 °C (104.4 °F) in [Kamianets-Podilskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamianets-Podilskyi).[^cite_note-484]

### United Kingdom

Main article: [2026 United Kingdom heatwaves](83274214.md)



Bottled water for commuters at [Leeds City railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leeds_railway_station)

On 25 May, the highest springtime temperature ever recorded in [Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Britain) was recorded in [Kew Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kew_Gardens) at 34.8 °C (94.6 °F), exceeding the previous record by over 2 °C (3.6 °F).[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-korosec-23][^cite_note-486] The next day on 26 May, the previous day's record was broken again at Kew Gardens with a reading of 35.1 °C (95.2 °F).[^cite_note-487][^cite_note-488] The heatwave has led to recorded 34.3 °C (93.7 °F) temperatures onboard [London Underground](17839.md) trains and stations,[^cite_note-489] and at least 15 people died in water-related incidents across the country.[^cite_note-490]

On 22 June, the [Met Office](319671.md) issued a red extreme heat warning for 24 and 25 June, the second time in history that such a warning has been issued.[^cite_note-491] The June temperature record was broken three days in a row from 24 to 26 June. On 24 June, the record was broken in [Gosport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gosport), [Hampshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampshire), with a reading of 36.1 °C (97.0 °F), on 25 June, in [Merryfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merryfield,_Ilton), [Somerset](51763.md), with a reading of 36.7 °C (98.1 °F) and on 26 June, in [Lingwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingwood), [Norfolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk), with a reading of 38.0 °C (100.4 °F).[^cite_note-492] The record before the heatwave was 35.6 °C (96.1 °F) set in 1957 and 1976.[^cite_note-493]

The extreme heat was also felt in the [Crown Dependencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Dependencies) of [Jersey](15693.md), [Guernsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey), and the [Isle of Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle_of_Man).[^cite_note-494] On 25 June, Jersey reached a temperature of 39.3 °C (102.7 °F). This is the highest temperature ever recorded on the island since records began in 1894.[^cite_note-495]

The record for the highest minimum temperature in [Wales](69894.md) for June was broken in [Cardiff](5882.md) on the night of 25 June, with a registered temperature of 23.5 °C (74.3 °F).[^cite_note-Carrington-6]

On 3 July, yellow heat warnings were issued for most of England starting on 4 July.[^cite_note-Davanos-33] On 6 July, the warnings in the South of England were upgraded to amber from 8 to 12 July.[^cite_note-bbc1-34] On 9 July, temperatures peaked at 35.5 °C (95.9 °F) in [Wisley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisley), making it the eighth day of the year when temperatures exceeded 34 °C (93 °F), breaking the previous record of seven set in 1976 and equalled in 2020.[^cite_note-496]

On 22 July, supermarkets warned there could be a food shortage later in the year, due to poor crop yields.[^cite_note-497]

From 28 to 30 July, another heatwave affected large parts of the UK, mainly in Southern England. Temperatures peaked at 35 °C (95 °F) in parts of South East England.[^cite_note-498]

On 30 July, a state of drought was declared across parts of Southern and Western England.[^cite_note-499] Due to the drought, a series of hosepipe bans were announced across Southern England in July and early August, affecting nearly 26 million people.[^cite_note-500]

On 7 August, the Met Office issued a series of high heat alerts across England starting from 9 August.[^cite_note-501] On 13 August, the UK recorded a temperature of 38.2 °C (100.8 °F) at [Hartpury College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hartpury_College), [Gloucestershire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloucestershire), which is the hottest day recorded in 2026 and the joint 4th hottest day ever recorded in the UK,[^cite_note-502] tied with a temperature recorded in [Pitsford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitsford), [Northamptonshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northamptonshire) on 18 July 2022.[^cite_note-503]

#### Subsequent wild fires

Due to the excessive heat, the number of wildfires has increased drastically, with 1,017 wildfires recorded as of 13 August 2026, according to the National Fire Chiefs Council (NFCC). This is as many wildfires as was recorded in all of 2025.[^cite_note-504] On 14 August, a temporary ban on [disposable barbecues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disposable_grill) was instigated,[^cite_note-505] and an [emergency alert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_Alerts_\(United_Kingdom\)) was sent to phones in England and Wales to inform people of the threat of wildfires.[^cite_note-506]

### The Vatican

The Vatican, a small enclave inside the city of Rome, was affected similarly to the rest of Rome.

## Analysis

### Climate implications

[Teresa Ribera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teresa_Ribera), a [European commissioner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_commission), blamed "ideologically driven" [falsehoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial) driven by people with interests in [fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel) for obstructing [EU green policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_the_European_Union#Climate_change).[^cite_note-519] [Climatologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climatologist) [Friederike Otto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friederike_Otto) commented that "it's climate change \[...] not [El Niño](317311.md)".[^cite_note-520] Scientists from the [World Weather Attribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Weather_Attribution) said that the heatwave was "virtually impossible" without climate change, and that the [concurrent El Niño](83914690.md) weather pattern "did not contribute" to the severe heat.[^cite_note-521]

The extreme heat set the backdrop for a special address by [UN secretary-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_secretary-general) [António Guterres](471806.md) at London Climate Week on 23 June:

> We have just lived through the eleven hottest years ever recorded. Climate disasters are becoming more frequent, more destructive, and more costly. And the World Meteorological Organization has warned we ain't seen nothing yet. El Niño is not just knocking on the door. It risks blowing the house down. Turning up the heat. Disrupting food and water systems. And hitting the vulnerable the hardest.[^cite_note-wmo-31]

### Infrastructure

Heat-induced bending of [rail tracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_track) and [catenary wires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catenary_wire) prompted many cancellations, particularly in France and Belgium.[^cite_note-F24Train-522] Much of Europe's train network was not designed for the high heat.[^cite_note-NierenbergNYT-523]

### Air conditioning

The [politicisation of air conditioning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_air_conditioning)[^cite_note-524] reignited, with right-wing politicians in multiple countries championing the technology and characterising resistance to its adoption as a misguided left-wing project. [Marine Le Pen](572897.md) vowed to "put into place a massive air-conditioning plan" if elected president of France;[^cite_note-ShearNYT-525] left-wing leader [Jean-Luc Melenchon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_Melenchon) instead declared, "I won't put my kid or my granddaughter or my great-granddaughter somewhere with air conditioning from morning to night!"[^cite_note-Mouterde-526] The centre-left government of [Ghent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghent) initially advised residents to "avoid air-conditioners", but removed the advice after an enquiry from the *[New York Times](30680.md)*.[^cite_note-ShearNYT-525] [CNN](62028.md) reported that mass adoption of air conditioning could set the European Union back from its goal of [climate neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_neutral) by 2050.[^cite_note-527] Energy cost also presents a significant barrier to wider adoption, and 38% of respondents to an EU-wide survey said they could not afford to cool their homes.[^cite_note-KueblerDW-528] Many train cancellations were prompted by a lack of air conditioning.[^cite_note-F24Trains-529] Because the windows of many trains do not open, a disabled train in high heat presents a dangerous situation as it may not occur to passengers to avail themselves of emergency [window breakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Window_breaker) to prevent deaths onboard.[^cite_note-NierenbergNYT-523]

The lack of air conditioning in some European countries baffled many Americans.[^cite_note-530] Similarly, Chinese social media contrasted the situation in Europe with the one in China, where air conditioning is so widely adopted that it is even used in barns and pig stalls.[^cite_note-FrayerNBC-531] Focusing on France, some commentators diagnosed a supposed French psychological propensity to embrace suffering, which led to recriminations that the prevalence of air conditioning in the United States evinced greed or decadence.[^cite_note-532][^cite_note-curbed-533] *[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde)* noted that air conditioning is widely viewed in France as a [maladaptation to climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_adaptation#Maladaptation), but that the 2026 heatwave engendered more public discussion of the role of air conditioning in the country.[^cite_note-Mouterde-526] French resistance toward air conditioning may be influenced by a [superstition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superstition) of *choc thermique* ("thermal shock"), the supposed tendency for air conditioning to cause fainting, nausea, or viral illness by virtue of the temperature differential moving from outdoors to indoors.[^cite_note-534][^cite_note-curbed-533] The superstition was promoted by French media organisations.[^cite_note-535] Furthermore, a superstition persists in Europe more widely that cold air causes illness;[^cite_note-536] in reality, illnesses such as [influenza](19572217.md) and [colds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colds) are caused by [viruses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viruses).[^cite_note-537]

### Excess mortality trends

Scientists at [Indiana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University) made preliminary estimates of [excess mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excess_mortality) related to the heatwave, based on data from past heatwaves such as the [2025 European heatwaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_European_heatwaves).[^cite_note-\:0-538] The previous 2025 heatwaves were associated with 16,500 excess deaths.[^cite_note-539] The team estimated that in the 2026 heatwaves were associated with 20,390 excess deaths (95% confidence interval: 17,201–25,141), but cautioned that estimates are subject to change.[^cite_note-540][^cite_note-\:0-538] An alternative preliminary estimate by a researcher at [Poznań University of Medical Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozna%C5%84_University_of_Medical_Sciences) puts the excess mortality at 15,000.[^cite_note-\:0-538] The European subcontinent has more heat deaths per capita than any other landmass.[^cite_note-541]
